# Mining log

## 2026-08-07 (wave 3) literature/own/ COMPLETE — all 14 transcribed, +7 cards

Overseer: "also transcribe then all our own ones", then "do these in this order" (cards, remaining
documents, citations). **All 14 own PDFs now carry a transcript and link in SHELF_INDEX** — up from
0 linked at the start of the day, when the index did not scan `own/` at all.

**Uneven coverage, by design, and every banner says which:** short notes statement-complete; the
85pp flagship and the 52pp Fu-BLM note structural + introduction-complete; the two slide decks
opening-slides only. `SydneyAlgebra25` (146pp) is now the largest unread item in `own/`.

**Seven cards** harvested: `own-habiro-le-even-hybrid-center-general-lattice-conjecture` ·
`own-weight-newton-basis-equals-lusztig-toral-lattice` · `own-sl3-habiro-le-even-center-conjecture` ·
`own-sl3-naive-plethystic-theta-not-baseline-integral` · `own-ktheory-as-center-of-quantum-category-o` ·
`own-fixed-point-localization-ktheory-and-bezrukavnikov-ring` · `own-sl2-rees-newton-centre-theorem`.

**Five findings.**

1. **Remark 0.20 of the flagship is the charter of our integral form** — the stated reason the whole
   Lusztig toral part is included, the even part taken, and Lusztig's divided powers *forced*. It is
   why the Lusztig cards on this shelf are load-bearing for us rather than background, and that link
   was written nowhere.
2. **A conjecture chain nobody had assembled:** flagship Conj 0.19 → our general-lattice conjecture
   → the sl₃ instance. **None of the three reconciliations has been performed**; all three cards say so.
3. **A proved lemma was sitting unindexed** — `N^X = T^{Lus}_X`, short and elementary, a
   formalization candidate. And a **third** description of the same rank-one lattice (Harman–Hopkins
   `T_{HH}`) is claimed proved elsewhere; **the pairs have never been chained**, and Harman–Hopkins
   is on our shelf unlinked and unread.
4. **The two orphan one-pagers now have homes:** `Kfiber.pdf` is Theorem 0.1 of the flagship,
   specialized; `TensorVermaFinite.pdf` is a plot in the ViaFiPoS Figure-2 plane.
5. **`bmpmxiproofs_v11` Definition 1.1 is the package's own hybrid `GL_n` presentation**, with the
   full Hopf structure and `[e_i,F_j] = δ_{ij}(K_i^+ − K_i^-)` — the convention `CLAUDE.md` requires
   everything to match — and it had no transcript. It also carries the cleanest one-line definition of
   "hybrid" on the shelf: Lacabanne's double before the Hopf quotient `K_iL_i^{-1} = 1`.

**Two independent notes agree on the remaining gap:** `bmpmxi` Conjecture 8.13 (finite-PBW
saturation) and Fu-BLM v100 §14 (the finite-PBW obstruction) are the same obstruction.

**Version hazard recorded:** the Fu-BLM PDF is v100 while `corpus/sources/fu_blm/` holds v104 — a
*later* revision, not the PDF's origin. Several `gl3-*` cards cite "v100/v104" and have **not** been
reconciled against the transcript.

## 2026-08-07 (wave 2) Our own papers — 6 of 12 transcribed

Overseer: "also transcribe then all our own ones". `literature/own/` holds 14 PDFs; 2 were already
transcribed (ViaFiPoS, Orsay26). **6 of the remaining 12 done this session; 6 queued.**

| done | pp | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| `own-sl3-habiro-le-even-center-conjecture` | 9 | **= goalv2.** The sl3 Habiro–Lê even centre conjecture + `Z ≅ K_{T×C^×}(Gr_{SL_3})`, with the Magma evidence AND the negative table |
| `own-habiro-le-even-hybrid-centers-explicit-torus` | 15 | **the programme's ROOT conjecture**, all lattices `Y ⊆ Λ ⊆ X`, completed form, geometric target; contains one genuinely PROVED lemma |
| `own-quantum-medium-algebras-ista-2024-notes` | 8 | handwritten ISTA 2.09.24 notes — the ViaFiPoS three-column picture PLUS a quantum column ViaFiPoS lacks |
| `own-plethystic-gkm-lifting-candidates` | 2 | the test-design note for the GKM lifting experiment |
| `own-kfiber-formula` | 1 | one displayed formula, no text layer, read by vision |
| `own-tensor-verma-finite-plot` | 1 | one Mathematica plot, no caption |

**Queued (6):** `Centers of quantum categories O … affine Grassmannian v.1.97 extended` (85pp,
flagship), `hybrid_fu_blm_v100` (52pp), `sl2_rees_newton_kostant_kumar_conjecture_v27` (28pp),
`bmpmxiproofs_v11` (20pp), `StonyBrook24` (26pp slides), `SydneyAlgebra25` (146pp beamer w/ overlays).

**Three findings.**

1. **The two Habiro–Lê notes are a general/instance pair and nobody had said so.** The 04-27 note
   states the conjecture for every `Y ⊆ Λ ⊆ X` in **completed** form with the intrinsic target
   `\widehat Z ≅ K^{top}_{(G^∨_Λ)_{ad}×C^×_q}(Gr_{G^∨_Λ}) ⊗̂ 𝒜^Λ_z`; the 04-26 note is its `sl₃`
   instance. **They have NOT been reconciled against each other** and both transcripts say so.
2. **A proved lemma was sitting unindexed:** `N^X = T^{Lus}_X` — the weight-Newton basis and the
   Lusztig divided-power basis span the same rank-one toral lattice, by a triangular
   change-of-basis with unit diagonal. Self-contained, with its proof, and a formalization
   candidate.
3. **The `sl₃` note's negative table is the argument for the whole repair**: naive plethystic theta
   classes pass GKM/fixed-point/centrality but fail baseline integrality, with bad-coefficient
   counts growing 0 → 4 → 30 → 196 → 778 with degree. That is what forces the Habiro–Lê even
   predicate over the older `IsInSL3HybridIntegralForm`.

**Recurring trap, same package, third sighting:** `e_{12}^{pkg} = (q − q^{−1})e_{12}^{HL}` in the sl3
note is the same non-unit rescaling already recorded for `gen_F12` on
`lit-lusztig-braid-symmetry-generator-formulas`. `q − q^{−1}` is not a unit in `𝒜_z`, so it changes
the lattice, and it has now bitten in two independent places.

## 2026-08-07 ViaFiPoS (ERC AdG 2024, Parts B1+B2) — transcribed into `literature/own/`, 5 cards

Overseer directive. The file had been sitting at the **corpus ROOT** as `ViaFiPos (49).pdf` — a
browser-download name, on no shelf — since 2025-07-05. Moved to
`literature/own/ViaFiPoS_ERC_AdG_2024_B1_B2.pdf`, transcribed
(`literature/transcribed/hausel-viafipos-erc-adg-2024.md`), 5 cards `own-hausel-*`. Born-digital
LaTeX, so read from the text layer — no vision pass needed.

**Scope decision, recorded in §0 of the transcript.** Part B1 §b is a **CV and track record** —
appointments, prizes, funding history, students, publication list. Not transcribed: it is not
mathematics, it is personal data about named individuals, and grant administration has its own home
in `hybrid/notes/` under the Grants seat. The PDF is on the shelf in full; what is declined is
*indexing a CV as searchable Library content*.

**The finding that matters: we do not hold the paper any of it rests on.** Every proved statement in
the proposal is attributed to `[Hau24c]` = **T. Hausel, *Commutative avatars of representations of
semisimple Lie groups*, to appear in PNAS 2024** — Theorems 1.1.3, 2.1, 2.3, 3.1. Verified absent
from the shelf: no transcript, no scan, no arXiv source. **We hold a grant proposal's one-line
summaries of four theorems, not the theorems.** All four cards carry that in `status_note`, and
`LR-20260807T-librarian-hausel-commutative-avatars` is filed. It is the PI's own paper, so the
fastest route is to ask.

**The one place ViaFiPoS touches this programme** is `[Hau24c, Thm 3.1]`: the medium algebra *is*
`H*_{G^∨}(Gr^μ)` and the big algebra *is* `IH*_{G^∨}(Gr^μ)` as a module. That is the classical,
`q`-free shadow of what the Sandbox studies over `A_z` — a degeneration target, not a citable
quantum result. Everything else (Hitchin systems, mirror branes) is carded for orientation and to
mark the boundary.

**A structural gap found while shelving it: `literature/own/` was never scanned by the index.** The
builder globbed `literature/pdf/` only, so **none of our own 14 PDFs could ever link to a
transcript** — including Orsay26, transcribed and carded since 2026-08-04 and recorded in
`own/SOURCES.md` as a *verified pair*. Fixed; both ViaFiPoS and Orsay26 now link, and 12 own/ PDFs
are now visibly transcript-less rather than invisibly so.

## 2026-08-06 (wave 3) Lusztig Ch 23-26 — FORMULA-COMPLETE, at ResidentA's request

ResidentA reported opening the PDF directly because the transcript "records their structure but not
all of the decisive formulas". Correct diagnosis: waves 1-2 were statement-complete but
display-selective. Wave 3 transcribes **every numbered item and every decisive display of Chapters
23, 24, 25 and 26** (printed 185-213 = PDF 200-228) into new transcript sections **3C and 3D**.
Transcript now 84 KB; coverage ~70 of 338 printed pages.

Recovered formulas most likely to have been the reason for reopening the PDF:

- **24.3.1, the partial order on `B x B`** — not a product order, not total; the "either/or" is what
  makes intervals finite, i.e. exactly Lemma 24.2.1's hypothesis.
- **Lemma 24.2.1 in full** (the abstract semilinear-system solver) with the `qbar + q = 0` step.
- **24.3.2**: `Psi(x) = Theta(xbar)`, semilinear, `Psi^2 = 1`.
- 23.1.5-23.1.8 (comultiplication into a **direct product**, the four (anti)automorphisms, Lemma
  23.1.7 with its explicit exponent `p`), 23.3.3 the left ideals `P(zeta,a,a')`, 23.3.5-23.3.10.
- 24.1.2-24.1.6 (the two `Theta` commutation displays; `p_{b,b'} in A`).
- 25.1.2-25.1.7 and 25.1.10, including the pairing `[,]` of 25.1.4 and the transition map `t`.
- Thm 25.2.5, Prop 25.2.6 (`b^-1_zeta = 1 <>_zeta b`, so `B-dot` generalizes `B`).
- **All of Chapter 26**, previously unread: Thm 26.1.2, 26.1.3-26.1.6, 26.2.1-26.2.3 (inner product
  as a limit), and **Thm 26.3.1**.

**Two corrections to my own earlier record.**

1. **Rank 1 was the wrong shape.** Wave 2 said the canonical basis of `U-dot` for `sl2` is
   `E^(a)F^(b)1_lambda` and `F^(b)E^(a)1_lambda`. Prop 25.3.2 actually gives
   `E_i^{(a)} 1_{-n} F_i^{(b)}` and `F_i^{(b)} 1_n E_i^{(a)}` with **`n >= a+b`** — idempotent
   **between** the divided powers, with 25.3.1(c) **identifying** the two families at `n = a+b`.
   Card `lit-lusztig-canonical-basis-bdot-construction` corrected.
2. Wave 2's "Ch 26 entirely unread" is superseded.

**The find of the wave: Thm 26.3.1(b).** `beta in B-dot union (-B-dot)` **iff** `beta in _A U-dot`,
`bar(beta) = beta`, and `(beta,beta) in 1 + v^{-1}A`. A recognition criterion with no reference to
`B`, to tensor products, or to the stabilization — the statement to imitate when asking whether a
proposed basis "is canonical". Ch 23-25 is the construction; this is the test.

## 2026-08-06 (wave 2) Lusztig Part IV — the idempotented algebra `U̇` and the construction of `Ḃ`

Overseer-directed deeper mining pass on the same book. **+5 cards (9 total), +1 query.** Transcript
gains a §3B covering the whole Part IV arc: *what `U̇` is (23.1) → canonical basis of a tensor
product (24.3) → stabilization into a basis of `U̇` (25.1–25.2) → what it buys (25.4, 29.3, 29.4)*.
Statement-complete coverage is now ~40 of 338 printed pages.

- **Cards:** `lit-lusztig-modified-algebra-udot-definition` ·
  `lit-lusztig-canonical-basis-bdot-construction` ·
  `lit-lusztig-canonical-basis-tensor-product-mechanism` ·
  `lit-lusztig-udot-structure-constants-positivity-conjecture` (status **open**) ·
  `lit-lusztig-udot-cells-and-refined-peter-weyl`.
- **Query:** `LQ-20260806-librarian-lusztig-conjecture-25-4-2-status`.

**Four findings.**

1. **The printed definition of the multiplication on `U̇` is not well-typed** (23.1.1, p. 185).
   `U(ν)` is `Z[I]`-graded, `λ′ − λ″` lives in `X`, and `Z[I] → X` need not be injective; the
   printed "`t ∈ U(λ′₁ − λ″₁)`" only parses when it is. The 2010 erratum repairs it by quantifying
   over `ν₁, ν₂ ∈ Z[I]` lifting the weight differences. **A Lean transcription of the printed page
   would silently assume injectivity.** Read at 450 dpi to settle it — and the erratum itself
   carries two apparent subscript slips, recorded as read and not adjudicated.
2. **`Ḃ` is built by bar-invariance + unitriangularity + a stabilization limit**, and the
   unitriangularity is `b◊_ζb″ ≡ b⁺b″⁻1_ζ mod P(tr|b|−1, tr|b″|−1)` — against the PBW-type basis of
   Lemma 23.2.2(b), for the *height* filtration. **This is the same flow-up/triangular-with-support
   shape the programme keeps rebuilding**, and it is the concrete use of the abstract mechanism
   already carded as `lit-lusztig-bar-unitriangular-canonicalization`.
3. **Conjecture 25.4.2 is OPEN in this book**: positivity `m^c_{ab}, m̂^{ab}_c ∈ N[v,v⁻¹]` for
   symmetric Cartan data, explicitly awaiting a perverse-sheaf model of `(U̇, Ḃ)`. **Integrality
   (`∈ 𝒜`) is a theorem and citable; positivity is not.** Status after 1993 deliberately not
   asserted — query filed instead.
4. **Thm 24.3.3(d) is verbatim the part the errata says is MISSING from Thm 27.3.2** (p. 220). The
   same `ℒ ∩ Ψ(ℒ) ≅ ℒ/v⁻¹ℒ` statement appears twice in the book and the second occurrence lost its
   part (d) in printing — and it is the *uniqueness argument*, not decoration.

## 2026-08-06 Lusztig, *Introduction to Quantum Groups* — acquired, transcribed, 4 cards

Overseer deposit, `corpus/literature/pdf/Lusztig Introduction to Quantum Groups.pdf`, sha256
`de1daa2e…43cd0e`, MBC 2010 reprint of the 1994 printing, 361 PDF pp. Transcript
`literature/transcribed/lusztig-introduction-to-quantum-groups.md`. Shelf index rebuilt: 242
transcripts, 118 scans, 60 transcript↔scan links.

- **Census PASSED**, `printed = PDF − 15`, content printed 1–338 = PDF 16–353, no gaps. Method:
  machine pass for the printed number in the head/foot window (314/339 hit), then **every one of
  the 25 misses inspected individually** — 15 carried the number elsewhere, the 10 genuinely
  unnumbered are the Part-title and "Notes on Part N" openers, each cross-checked against its
  TOC-declared page. Cheaper than contact sheets and it cross-validates against a second source.
- **Scan with a destructive OCR layer** (`Q(v)`→`Q(t;)`, `E_i ∈ U(i)`→`Ei G U(«)`, `Σ_{λ∈X}`→
  `Y^xex`). Read by vision, per the banked rule. The layer *is* good enough to lift the TOC and to
  page-census — locating vs reading is the line.
- **Cards:** `lit-lusztig-braid-pbw-integral-basis` (41.1.4 + 41.1.7) ·
  `lit-lusztig-pbw-integrality-provenance-chain` (the two mis-pointers) ·
  `lit-lusztig-adot-integral-form` (23.2.2, 23.2.4) ·
  `lit-lusztig-braid-symmetry-generator-formulas` (37.1.3, 41.1.2, the A₂ specialization).
- **Disposed** `LR-20260803T145920Z-residentA2` with a correction to its premise; notified
  residentA2, hypervisorv2, BUILD.

**Three findings worth carrying forward.**

1. **41.1.3 is not the PBW basis theorem.** It gives integrality of *one* braid-translated divided
   power. The 𝒜-basis is **41.1.4**; "the span is all of `𝒜U⁺`" is **41.1.7(a)**. Habiro–Lê
   Prop 5.2(b) credits "[54, Prop 41.1.3]" and is one proposition short — and that pointer had
   already propagated into a sandboxA2 manuscript.
2. **The 2010 reprint's errata corrects the book's own citation**: *"p. 325 line −10: replace [7]
   by [6]"* — i.e. the proof of 41.1.4 is in *Quantum groups at roots of 1* (Geom. Dedicata 35),
   not *Canonical bases* (JAMS 3). **We already hold [6] transcribed**, so the chain terminates
   inside the Library. Both are Lusztig 1990 and both are on our shelf, so the wrong pointer
   resolves to a real card and fails silently.
3. **A non-unit convention gap in our own package.** CAS-measured: `HybridQuantum.A2.gen_F12` =
   `(F₂F₁ − qF₁F₂)/(q − q⁻¹)`, while Lusztig's braid root vector and the manuscript's `F₁₂` agree
   up to the unit `−q`. `q − q⁻¹` is not a unit in `Z[q,q⁻¹]`, so the package generator spans a
   different lattice. Two docstrings in `HQ/src/A2/generators.jl` assert the un-rescaled relations
   and are stale by exactly that factor. Reported to BUILD; not edited (AD-3).

## 2026-07-13 Orsay26 / lambda-ribbon completion synthesis

- Added `sources/center/completed_even_hybrid_center_lambda_ribbon_generation_strategy_2026-07-13.tex`, a TeX evaluation of the proposed formula `Zhat_hyb^ev = closure(A_z[E_1,E_2,...,V,V^-1])` using the Orsay26 affine-Hecke argument and the corpus power-sum identity.
- Added the conjecture card `completed-even-center-lambda-ribbon-generation` and indexed it. The source proves a conditional tensor-window theorem: integral lambda classes recover the elementary symmetric Bernstein operators unitriangularly, the inverse ribbon supplies the invertible determinant, and inverse-limit density is formal once cumulative finite-window generation is known.
- Kept four proof obligations explicit: integral lambda lift, integral ribbon/balancing identity, arithmetic plus simultaneous finite-window saturation, and cofinality of tensor windows with the canonical matrix-coefficient/Schubert completion. The canonical boxed formula therefore remains **OPEN**.
- Recorded two convention traps: Orsay's finite `E_n(u)` is not the stable package class `E_m`, and the slides' `C[v^+-]` integrality is not the required `Z[q^+-,z^+-]` saturation.

  transposition — `B_2` is required. A probe that passes on a degenerate case is worse than no probe:
  it manufactures false confidence. This is the program's "locks need separating witnesses" lesson
  (v1 drift postmortem) arriving independently in the Library layer. **Future mining prompts must ask
  for the separating witness explicitly, not just a bounded probe.**

  `\\`) dropped by the transcriber; (b) `$`-delimiter stripping when the source used inline math;
  (c) commented-out material the transcript DELIBERATELY preserves (my normalizer strips comments
  from the source side, so these can never match); (d) block re-segmentation. Raw rates were
  FFNR 95% / BF 95% / Tsymbaliuk 86% -> 97% once (b) is tolerated; **FT partD 32% -> 75%** and
  **the prior seat's FT partC 61%** once (a)/(c) are tolerated. The FT parts are the weak case and
  the residual there is genuine reformatting, not byte-exactness. **Consequence: for the FT merge
  the referee must carry the fidelity load; do not declare the FT parts mechanically verified.**

  A.25->**A.6** · `Derivation 2` A.28->**A.7**. 46 occurrences rewritten, 0 stale, changelog appended.
- TWO INDEPENDENT CORROBORATIONS that the new mapping is right, not just different: (i) the corrected
  sequence A.1,A.2,A.3,[A.4],A.5,A.6,A.7 is CONTIGUOUS and the single `.aux` gap (A.4, no `\newlabel`)
  falls exactly on partC's one *unlabeled* Remark; (ii) partC's **Appendix B** numerals (B.2/B.5/B.6)
  were ALREADY CORRECT per `.aux` and needed no change — the error is localized precisely to
  Appendix A, which is exactly where the `\tag`s occur.
- partC's `[sic]` on the absence of `v17` independently RE-VERIFIED and UPHELD (`grep`: zero
  `\tag{v17}`, both `v16` and `v18` present) — a genuine numbering gap in the paper itself.
- **LESSON TO BANK — PREFER `.aux` OVER COUNTER-SIMULATION WHENEVER THE ARXIV SOURCE SHIPS ONE.**
  T8 established "simulate the LaTeX theorem counters" as the mechanized-referee numbering check.
  That is the right method only when there is no compile output. When `arxiv_src/<id>/` contains a
  `.aux` (or `.toc`), `\newlabel` gives the PRINTED number directly and is strictly stronger than any
  simulation — simulation silently inherits every wrong assumption about `\tag`, starred
  environments, and shared counters. Amend the mechanized-referee recipe accordingly.
- **AND THE PROCESS LESSON:** partC was honest — it marked every one of these
  `{UNCERTAIN — computed numeral}` and said it had not checked the PDF. The honest uncertainty flag
  is what made the contradiction findable and cheap to fix. It also means a transcript can be
  100% byte-exact on formulas and still ship wrong statement NUMBERS; numbering is a separate axis
  from fidelity and needs its own ground-truth check. Cross-part disagreement is a FEATURE of the
  A/B/C/D split — instruct split-transcription seats to flag contradictions with siblings explicitly.

## 2026-07-25 — T10, Cameron–Vinroot (arXiv:1305.2404v1). Acquisition + transcription + mining, one pass.

**Trigger.** Overseer summoned the Librarian in-session and named the paper. Intake
(`br_intake.py --channel librarian`) showed **5 untriaged LR requests, none previously triaged** —
four from `residentroot` (2026-07-25T15:10:4[0-3]Z) and one from `residentA2`
(2026-07-25T15:38:33Z). The named paper **is** the residentA2 request, so this wave disposes that
one; **the four residentroot requests remain UNTRIAGED** and are the standing queue.

**Scope decision.** 10 pages, single `.tex`, no vision needed → transcribed **inline by the seat**
rather than dispatched. At this size, statement-, proof- and display-**complete** is cheaper than
selective, so no completeness gap had to be disclosed or defended.

**Delivered.** `transcribed/cameron-vinroot-multivariate-rogers-szego.md` (39/39 displays, 9/9
statements, 7/7 proofs, page anchors from the PDF); four cards promoted to `statements/` —
`lit-cameron-vinroot-multivariate-rs-product-formula`,
`lit-cameron-vinroot-rogers-szego-symmetric-basis`,
`lit-cameron-vinroot-prop23-ordinary-multinomial` (convention-trap),
`lit-cameron-vinroot-no-general-theta-closed-form` (negative). Shelf **123 → 124** transcripts,
cards **104 → 108** (counts reconciled against `ls`, per the standing don't-trust-memory rule).
**ROUTED same day** on the Overseer's in-session authorization — dated amendment at the foot of
`CARD_ROUTING_2026-07-17.md`, to `sandboxA2`/hypervisorv2 + residentA2. **No referee pass run** — owed.

### What was learned

- **THE BANKED "API-CHECK EVERY arXiv ID" RULE PAID FOR ITSELF AGAIN, IN A NEW DIRECTION.** The
  check confirmed title/authors/date but also established there is **no journal reference and no
  DOI** on the record. The requesting seat's citation gave none either, but a future card could
  easily have acquired a phantom venue. Recorded as "PREPRINT" in the transcript header, all four
  cards, and the roster. *Generalization: the API check is not only an id-validity check; it is the
  only cheap source of the venue/DOI fields that cards later assert.*
- **NEW HARVEST SHAPE — the bare gzipped `.tex`.** `arxiv.org/e-print/1305.2404` returns a
  **gzip-compressed single file**, not a tar archive; `file` reports `was "RSProduct.tex"`. `tar xz`
  fails on it. Use `gunzip -c src.tar.gz > <original-name>.tex`. Older single-file submissions take
  this shape; the shelf's existing `arxiv_src/` dirs are mostly tarballs, so this is a real variant
  to expect, not a one-off.
- **NO `.aux` SHIPS WITH A SINGLE-FILE E-PRINT — so use the PDF as numbering ground truth.** T9
  banked "prefer `.aux` over counter-simulation". That rule has a hole: single-file submissions have
  no compile output at all. **The PDF is the fallback ground truth and it is just as strong** —
  `pdftotext -layout` plus a per-page scan of statement headers and `(\d\.\d)` numerals confirmed
  9/9 statements and 6/6 equations directly, without simulating a single counter. Cheap, mechanical,
  and it verifies the *printed* number, which is what a citation asserts.
- **FOUR INDEPENDENT `\newtheorem` COUNTERS PRODUCE COLLIDING NUMBERS — a citation hazard the census
  must surface.** This source declares `theorem`/`lemma`/`prop`/`corollary` on four **separate**
  `[section]` counters, so §2 has both a **Proposition 2.1** and a **Theorem 2.1**, and §3 has both
  a **Corollary 3.1** and a **Theorem 3.1**. A card citing "2.1" is ambiguous and a reader following
  it lands on the wrong statement. *New standing check: after the `\newtheorem` census, ask whether
  the counters are SHARED (CK-style, `[Theorem]`) or INDEPENDENT, and if independent, list the
  colliding numbers in the transcript header and force the environment word into every citation.*
- **THE §5 DICTIONARY FAILED AGAIN — THIS TIME IT WOULD HAVE BEEN *MINE*.** The requester asked for
  the `q → Q=q²` normalization. Writing it from recall would have given the symmetric bracket; the
  paper's is the **one-sided** Gaussian one, and the true conversion is
  `binom{n}{r}_Q = q^{r(n-r)} [n choose r]_q` — **quadratic in the lower index**, hence *not*
  absorbable by any rescaling `t_i ↦ c_i t_i`. I derived it and then verified it symbolically before
  writing it down. Five consecutive waves now with the dictionary as the failure point. *The rule
  generalizes past refereeing others: when the SEAT ITSELF writes a conversion, machine-check it in
  the same breath.*
- **SYMBOLIC RE-DERIVATION IS A FIDELITY TEST FOR RECURSIONS, NOT JUST A SANITY CHECK.** I
  reimplemented θ from the transcribed recursion (1.2) and reproduced Theorem 2.1 (all `0≤k,n≤3` at
  `l=3`) and all four closed-form Propositions. A single wrong exponent or index in (1.2) breaks
  every one of those. *For any source whose content is a recursion or a structure-constant
  definition, re-derivation is strictly stronger than byte-diffing the display — it tests the
  formula's MEANING, and byte-diffing cannot.*
- **A MISSING `_q` THAT IS NOT A TYPO.** Proposition 2.3 mixes an **ordinary** multinomial with
  `q`-objects three symbols apart. Two independent confirmations: the proof closes on the ordinary
  Pascal recursion (false for `q`-multinomials), and the separating witness
  `l=3, m=[1,1], k=2, n=4` computes to the ordinary value `2·binom{4}{3}_q(q)_3`, refuting the
  `q`-reading `(1+q)·…`. Carded as `convention-trap` so nobody "fixes" it. *This is the mirror image
  of the usual trap: normally we protect against a transcript inventing a distinction; here we
  protect against a reader erasing a real one.*
- **DEGENERATE-WITNESS INVENTORY, WRITTEN INTO THE TRANSCRIPT.** Three traps that would each
  manufacture false confidence: `l=2` reproduces the classical formula exactly (Prop 2.4) — so the
  **certified GL₂ route is a regression test and CANNOT validate the GL₃ transport**, since it has
  no `e_3` at all; `q=1` kills every off-pivot coefficient (`H̃_n|_{q=1} = e_1^n`), so no probe at
  `q=1` can detect anything; and any `m⃗` with one nonzero coordinate cannot separate ordinary from
  `q`-multinomial. *Naming the degenerate cases alongside each witness is now cheap habit and it
  caught the GL₂-cannot-validate-GL₃ point, which is the single most useful line in the delivery.*
- **CARD THE ABSENCE WHEN THE REQUEST HOPED FOR MORE THAN THE PAPER HAS.** The request hoped the
  paper "may supply the missing all-degree unitriangular/straightening step". Half true: Lemma
  3.1 + Theorem 3.1 do, but for `Λ[q]` (polynomial variables, no torus, no invertibility, no
  `W`-invariance), and the source states outright that **no general closed form for θ exists**. That
  became its own `negative-result-scope-limit` card rather than a caveat buried in a traps list, so
  the next seat cannot re-hope. *Preserve-negatives applies to what a paper does not contain, not
  only to refuted mathematics.*
- **SAY WHICH HALF OF A REQUEST YOU COULD NOT CLOSE, AND WHY.** Item 5 asked whether dividing by the
  "standard Q-rise" yields `A_z` coefficients with unit pivots. The `Q`-rise and the lower-shell
  basepoint correction are **project-side objects with no counterpart in the source**, so the
  Library cannot close that half from this paper at any effort. Stated plainly in the transcript
  (§5a.4), the changelog, the roster, and the disposition — rather than delivering the determinate
  half and letting silence imply completeness.

### 2026-07-25 (later) — T10 FIDELITY PASS: "complete" had been mistaken for "exact"

Overseer asked whether the transcription was finished. Re-checked on disk rather than answering
from memory — and the check found something. **All 39 displays were present (complete), but ten
had been TIDIED rather than transcribed** (sub/superscript order, `\to` for `\rightarrow`,
`\varprojlim` for `\lim_{\longleftarrow}`, `\substack` for the source's `\atop`, terminal
punctuation, 16 inserted `\,` thin spaces, one bolded `[sic]` subscript). No symbol, index,
exponent or coefficient was affected and every symbolic check still passes — but the header claimed
"verbatim". Fixed; now **39/39 exact** by per-display `difflib` match. Changelog in-transcript.

- **SLOT-PRESENCE IS NOT FIDELITY — a new false-alarm mechanism, and this one ran the other way.**
  The wave's own completeness census (`D01…D39` all present) returned a clean 39/39 and was
  **worthless as a fidelity check**: it is satisfied by a tidied display just as well as an exact
  one. Every previously banked completeness heuristic failed by making a GOOD transcript look bad;
  this one failed by making an IMPERFECT transcript look perfect, which is strictly more dangerous.
  **On a SOURCE-based transcript, run the exactness test (one normalized `difflib` pass per display)
  BEFORE the header is allowed to say "verbatim".** It costs one script.
- **The transcriber's own tidying is a blind spot the referee would not have caught either.** The
  changes were all *improvements* by ordinary typesetting taste — which is exactly why they slipped
  past. A judgment referee reads for mathematics and would have passed them.
- **First-pass block-grouping false alarm, resolved not reported** (the fifth distinct mechanism in
  this Library's tally): grepping for `**D23 ` through `**D29 ` reported them MISSING; they are in
  fact present, grouped under one `**D23–D29**` heading with per-display inline tags. Resolved with
  the real test before it entered a report, per the standing rule.

## 2026-07-26 — the Library becomes three shelves: `preprints/` and `notes/` established

Overseer order: extend the Library from one shelf to three, and deposit what THIS programme
produced. Design: `architecture/decisions/AD-14-results-catalogue-proposal.md` (Addendum).
Input was a **manifest**, not a tree walk: `architecture/manifests/RESIDENT_SHELF_INVENTORY_2026-07-26.json`.

**Pass 1 (skeletons) + Pass 2 (31 preprints) DONE. Pass 3 (396 notes) staged, not started.**

- `preprints/` — `SP-2026-001…031`, backfill numbered by lexicographic source path (reproducible:
  `deposit.py plan-preprints`); after backfill, next-free from `SERIES.json`, never recomputed.
- `notes/` — `SN-<date>-<ownertag>-<loc>-<slug>`, every part derived from the source. **396 files →
  396 distinct ids, zero collisions**, measured.
- Tooling: `corpus/tools/{deposit,extract_tex,pass2_preprints}.py`. The tool refuses to write outside
  `corpus/`, refuses to overwrite a version, refuses a hash mismatch, and re-hashes the deposited
  bytes.

### What was learned

- **THE HASH GATE IS NOT CEREMONIAL — IT CAUGHT A FILE BEING WRITTEN UNDER US, LIVE.** One of 463
  manifest entries drifted, and it drifted *again during the session*
  (`d1777211010c075d` → `c7af57630a0a35ba` → `a63063d9df41b23c`): a sandboxA1c session minute that
  someone was actively editing. It waits, automatically. Had the deposit been a tree walk instead of
  a manifest-plus-hash-check, it would have pinned a snapshot that was already stale when taken and
  called it an edition. *The manifest is not paperwork; it is the thing that makes a deposit an
  edition rather than a guess.*
- **THE OWNERS' RECORDS ARE ALMOST ENTIRELY SILENT — 29 OF 31 MANUSCRIPTS CARRY NO GRADE ANYWHERE.**
  Measured, not assumed: every basename grepped against its owner's whole tree (`SandboxA/**` 4,360
  files; `sandboxA2/**` 2,384; `sandboxA1c/**` 702), `resident/` excluded because that is the
  Resident's shelf and not the owner's record. Exactly two hits, both in a `STATE.md`. **This is the
  single most important thing the deposit surfaced**: it was invisible while the manuscripts sat on
  shelves, and it is unmissable now that 19 cards say `unstated — owner query filed`. A catalogue's
  first service is not search; it is making an absence countable.
- **A GRADE CAN BE NARROWER THAN THE FILE IT NAMES.** hypervisorv2's record grades
  `established-informal` to **Thm 13.10 inside** an 88 kB manuscript, not to the manuscript. Recorded
  at exactly that scope (`SCOPED: Thm 13.10 / L2b only`) rather than promoting it to the document.
  *Copying a status means copying its scope; widening a scope is inventing a status.*
- **AN ELLIPSIS IS NOT AN ENUMERATION.** hypervisorA's STATE grants `candidate` to
  "`GL4_EVEN_HYBRID…tex`, `resident/GLN_… .tex`". The named file took the grade; **none** of the 12
  `GLN_*` manuscripts did, because "…" does not identify them. Asking cost one query; guessing would
  have manufactured 12 grades from a typographic convenience.
- **ONE GRADE CAME FROM A SIGNED RULE RATHER THAN AN OWNER'S LINE, AND THAT NEEDED SAYING OUT LOUD.**
  `candidate_*` files are `candidate` under AD-8 item 10 (Overseer-signed). That is not an inference
  — but the rule grades the *naming*, not the mathematics, so every such card records which it is and
  the owner queries ask for confirmation anyway.
- **8 OF 31 MANUSCRIPTS DECLARE NO CONVENTION FRAME.** Cards read `frame: UNSTATED IN SOURCE` and
  warn against transporting formulas out of them. Frames are **quoted with line anchors, never
  paraphrased** — the Librarian extracts and attributes; it does not restate mathematics it has not
  verified.
- **PUT THE CARDS WHERE THE SERVER ALREADY LOOKS.** The single highest-leverage design decision:
  cards go in `corpus/statements/`, which `library_search` globs and `get_card` resolves, so the new
  series is searchable **today with zero server change** — 31/31 verified reachable. The server work
  (`KIND_DIRS`, `shelf_status`, `get_card`'s field allowlist) is then a real but non-blocking BR
  instead of a prerequisite. *Design the deposit around the instrument you have, then ask for the
  instrument you want.*
- **`get_card` SILENTLY DROPS 12 FIELDS FOR ANY NON-LITERATURE CARD** — including `status_source` and
  `anchor_sha256`, the two a consumer needs to check that a grade is real and a deposit still matches
  its source. The branch is `status.startswith("literature")`. Mitigated by repeating everything in
  the card **body**, which is served in full; BR filed. Note this already affects pre-existing cards
  like `gl3-single-row-support-wall-law` — not a new-series defect.
- **A BAD QUERY LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE AN EMPTY SHELF.** My first acceptance probe returned 0 hits and
  for a moment read as "the deposits are not searchable". The search is a literal regex line match and
  my query was a phrase that appears nowhere verbatim. *Before reporting an instrument as broken,
  check the probe — this is the liveness lesson in a new costume.*
- **INSTRUMENT DUTY, honestly reported: `mcp__library__*` was NOT ATTACHED to this session.** The
  acceptance test was run by importing `engine/librarymcp/server.py` and calling the same tool
  functions directly — the same code path, but it is not the façade and should not be reported as if
  it were. AD-14 §5 measured 8 `mcp__library__*` calls across 3,319 transcripts; the reason is now
  concrete rather than cultural — in this session it was unreachable.

### 2026-07-26 (pass 2b) — the searchable renderings the deposits were missing

Overseer confirmed the order I proposed after I reported the gap: **render the 31 deposits, then the
6 outstanding literature transcriptions, then pass 3.** Pass 2b is done.

**Delivered:** `preprints/SP-2026-nnn/v1/SP-2026-nnn-v1.md` × 31 — **343 numbered statements and
3,458 displays, every one verbatim with a source line anchor**, plus frame, section map and anchor
table. 660 kB. Tool: `corpus/tools/render_preprint.py`.

**What these are and are not, stated at the top of every file:** a MECHANICAL VERBATIM rendering,
legitimate here because our sources are already TeX — no OCR layer, no reading judgement between
source and rendering. **Not** a mining-grade transcript: no notation dictionary reconciled against
our conventions, no traps, no relevance hooks, no referee. Those need per-manuscript mathematical
judgement and remain owed.

### What was learned

- **THE CENSUS GATE FIRED ON ITS FIRST RUN AND IT WAS *MY* DENOMINATOR THAT WAS WRONG.** 6 of 31
  renderings were REFUSED for census imbalance. Cause: `body.count("\\[")` counts LaTeX's
  **line-break-with-spacing** `\\[6pt]` as a display open. The captures were right all along; the
  count they were checked against was wrong. Fixed with a `(?<!\\)` lookbehind on both sides.
  *This is the naive-`\begin{...}`-grep lesson in a new costume — the fifth time a naive count has
  produced a false alarm in this Library, and the first time it did so inside a gate I had just
  written to prevent exactly that.* Refusing to write is the right default: it turned my bug into
  6 refusals instead of 6 silently-short renderings.
- **BUILD THE GATE SO IT REFUSES, NOT SO IT WARNS.** The renderer will not write a file whose census
  does not balance. So "a rendering exists" *means* "its statement and display counts equal the
  source's" — a reader never has to trust a claim in a header.
- **I FILED A BR ASKING BUILD TO IMPLEMENT MY OWN MISTAKE, AND CAUGHT IT ONLY WHEN THE OVERSEER
  ASKED A PLAIN QUESTION.** BR Ask 1 requested the search glob be widened to `.tex`. Wrong: the glob
  is `**/*.md` and the correct fix was on MY side — produce `.md` renderings, which is what "a
  searchable rendering" meant in the order all along. Amendment filed withdrawing that half.
  *A BR is a request for someone else's labour; getting it wrong spends their time, not mine.*
- **I POLLUTED THE INDEX WITH MY OWN DOCUMENTATION.** Adding a "Search keywords" block to each card,
  I included an explanatory sentence containing the example phrase "dressed cone" — so all 31 cards
  then matched that query instead of the one manuscript named for it. Caught by re-running the very
  probe that motivated the fix. **Rationale belongs in the README; only data belongs in an indexed
  card.** Corrected to data-only lines.
- **THE FINDABILITY DEFECT THAT PROMPTED IT IS REAL AND WORTH KEEPING.** `GLN_DRESSED_CONE_…tex` was
  not returned by a search for "dressed cone": the filename is `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE`, and neither the
  title nor the body prose repeats the phrase. The manuscripts are named in a vocabulary their own
  text does not use. Every card now carries the tokenised filename and the title. Verified: seven
  natural-language probes each now resolve to the correct manuscript.
- **`MAX_HITS = 40` — "found in N files" from a hit list is a CAP ARTIFACT, never coverage.** I twice
  nearly reported a capped hit list as a coverage measurement. Count coverage off the files, not off
  the search response.
- **Search is case-insensitive only when the query is entirely lowercase** (`server.py:281`).
  A mixed-case query silently becomes case-sensitive — worth knowing before reading a zero as absence.

### 2026-07-26 (pass 2c) — PDFs beside every deposit, and the incomplete-deposit defect they exposed

Overseer: put the PDF of each preprint in its folder, without auxiliary files. Done — **31 PDFs,
427 pages, zero auxiliary files in `corpus/`**, compiled by `corpus/tools/build_pdfs.py` in a
temporary tree outside the Library with only the `.pdf` copied back.

**A DEPOSIT WAS INCOMPLETE AND ONLY THE COMPILE FOUND IT.** `SP-2026-014`'s master `.tex`
`\input{}`s three files under `data/` on the Resident shelf. The original deposit copied only the
master, so the "frozen edition" was **the manuscript minus 23.5 kB of itself** — 14 numbered
statements and 72 displays absent from the record, and a document that could not be compiled or read
in full.

- **Nothing else would have caught it.** The hash gate verified the master byte-for-byte and was
  right to; completeness of a *multi-file document* is a different axis from fidelity of *a file*.
  The census gate compared the rendering against the master, so it balanced — against the wrong
  denominator. It took an Overseer request for PDFs, and a compile that broke, to surface it.
- **The renderer had asserted the opposite in writing.** Every rendering carried "any `\input`
  (none of these sources use `\input`)" — a claim I never computed, inherited from glancing at a
  couple of sources. *A transcript can assert completeness it does not have* is already banked
  twice in this file from T9; this is the third time, and the first where the false claim was **my
  own**. It is now computed: the renderer resolves `\input`/`\include` recursively and lists what it
  spliced. Statements 343 → 357, displays 3458 → 3530.
- **Gate added so it cannot recur:** `deposit.py` now refuses any deposit whose source `\input{}`s a
  file that is not also deposited.
- **v1 was CORRECTED IN PLACE rather than superseded by v2, deliberately.** A version number tracks
  the *manuscript*, not the Librarian's packaging: issuing v2 would tell every future reader the
  mathematics was revised today, which is false. v1 was hours old, cited by nothing, and its routing
  had not been delivered. Recorded loudly in `DEPOSIT_CORRECTION.json` with the reasoning, rather
  than hidden behind a version bump. **The master's bytes were not touched** — its sha256 is
  unchanged.
- **Derived artifacts get their own record.** `DEPOSIT.json` is the pin and is never rewritten;
  `DERIVED.json` carries the `.pdf` and `.md` hashes **plus the source hash they were built from**,
  so PDF↔source correspondence is provable, not assumed. Compiling from the frozen bytes (rather
  than copying a PDF already sitting on a Resident shelf) is the same discipline: a shelf PDF may
  have been built from a different revision.

## 2026-07-27 — T12 rendered: the five acquired papers, and the false negative closed

Working `architecture/specs/LIBRARY_READINESS_2026-07-27.md` in its stated order.
**T12 was acquired on 26 July and logged only in the roster — `grep -c T12` returned zero in this
journal and in the memory file.** The architect found that; it is fixed here and below.

**Item 1 — the deposit door.** `deposit-preprints` / `deposit-notes` now exist in `deposit.py`,
whose own docstring had advertised them since the day it was written. Works off the LIVE file, not
the 26 July manifest: a plan going stale is not a reason to pin the wrong bytes, so drift is
recorded on the deposit and the card rather than blocking a Resident. Card rendering extracted to
`cards.py` so the door and the backfill emit identical cards. Pass 3 stays gated (AD-15 §5).

**Item 2 — five papers rendered**, 225 statements and 505 displays, verbatim, census-enforced:
Al Qasimi–Stokman (47/117) · Korotkikh (63/88) · Lacabanne–Vaz (30/62) ·
Poulain d'Andecy–Zaimi (40/148) · Gunna–Yeliussizov (45/90). Shelf 124 → 129 transcripts.
Each carries a **LAYER** banner: mechanical rendering, curation owed — no §5 dictionary against our
conventions, no traps, no relevance hooks, no referee. Closing a false negative before the curation
lands is worth doing; pretending the curation has landed is not.

### What was learned

- **A COMMENTED-OUT `%\begin{lemma}` SILENTLY CORRUPTED A CAPTURE, AND ONLY THE CENSUS SAW IT.**
  `arXiv:1710.04058` line 434 is `%\begin{lemma}`; the capture regex matched from there to the next
  `\end{lemma}` **1,191 lines later**, swallowing the real lemma and emitting one corrupt block. The
  census reported 1 vs 2 and refused the file. Fixed by stripping comments *before* capture, with
  line numbers preserved so anchors stay true. **The counts on three papers I had rendered minutes
  earlier changed once the fix landed** (31→30, 42→40, 47→45 statements) — those first renderings
  were carrying corrupt blocks and would have shipped.
- **THE SAME HAZARD IS PRESENT ON 32 OTHER SHELF SOURCES**, some heavily: `2601.06696` has 102
  commented-out `\begin{}`, FT `1708.01795` has 34, `math0609110` 18, `math9812053` 16. **The
  mechanized-referee method banked in T8/T9 — "diff every fenced block against the source", "check
  every source display environment for presence" — does not strip comments**, so any census it ran
  over those sources could have been inflated the same way. Not a claim that those transcripts are
  wrong; a claim that the *check* on them was weaker than recorded. Worth a re-run before any of them
  is called mechanically verified again.
- **VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED, THAT THE 31 PREPRINTS WERE UNAFFECTED:** zero commented-out `\begin{}` in
  the deposited sources, and re-rendering gives byte-identical counts (357/3530).
- **TWO OF THE SPEC'S FOUR "ZERO" TOPICS WERE NEVER A TRANSCRIPTION PROBLEM.** `library_search` is a
  **literal per-line regex**: a multi-word query matches only if the words sit adjacently on one
  line. `annulus skein` returned nothing while `skein category of the annulus` returned the paper.
  And **`equivariant K-theory Schubert` returns nothing while `equivariant K-theory` returns 90 hits
  and `Schubert` 378** — that material was always on the shelf, and no amount of transcribing could
  ever have fixed that query. Alias blocks added to the five new transcripts; the general defect is
  a BR ask, and the architect's item-2 diagnosis needs this correction.
- **I REPEATED A LESSON I BANKED YESTERDAY, WITHIN ONE DAY.** The alias blocks initially carried a
  shared explanatory sentence containing the example phrase `annulus skein`, so **all five files
  matched it** — the identical self-pollution as the "dressed cone" incident on the preprint cards.
  Caught by re-running the probe. *Banking a lesson is not the same as having internalised it; the
  defence that actually worked, both times, was re-running the measurement after the fix.*
  **Rule, restated: rationale goes in the README; only data goes in an indexed file.**

## 2026-07-27 (T13) — the request tray answered: 10 papers acquired, 14 transcripts rendered

Tray had grown 11 → 16 overnight; 5 new requests, 11 named papers. All ids API-checked before
anything entered the record. **Tray now 0 untriaged.** Shelf 129 → 138 transcripts.

Acquired + rendered: Appel–Toledano Laredo 2208.05331 · Brundan–Savage–Webster 1812.04779 ·
Lambropoulou math/0405504 · Geck–Lambropoulou math/0405508 · Orellana math/9806163 ·
Martel 2002.08785 · Martel–Willetts 2112.15204 · Anghel–Murakami 2606.19113v1 · Morton 0707.2851.
Independently recounted off the sources: 14/14 balanced, 28/28 sampled statements byte-exact.

### What was learned

- **THE CENSUS GATE HAD A HOLE: 0 == 0 BALANCES.** Orellana–Ram (math/0401317) rendered **0
  statements and 0 displays and PASSED** — because the gate only asks whether captured equals
  source, and both were zero. The cause is real: that paper is **plain TeX**, not LaTeX
  (`\magnification`, `\input prepictex`, no `\documentclass`, no `\newtheorem`), so a LaTeX-shaped
  extractor matches nothing by construction. **An empty transcript is worse than no transcript: it
  looks like coverage.** Zero-capture guard added; the empty file deleted; everything re-run.
  *A gate that only checks internal consistency will pass a pipeline that read nothing at all.*
- **A DOI-ONLY REQUEST IS STILL AN API-CHECKABLE REQUEST.** Brundan–Savage–Webster was filed with a
  DOI and no arXiv id. Title search found 1812.04779 and the `journal_ref` field confirmed the match
  (same authors, same volume/pages). Do not treat "no arXiv id given" as "not on arXiv".
- **MY OWN VERIFICATION GREP HIT A COMMENTED-OUT LINE.** Checking that each source matched its
  requested title, `grep -m1 '\title{'` on Martel 2002.08785 returned
  `%\title{Analyse et contrôle de modèles d'écoulements fluides}` — a commented-out leftover from a
  different document — and for a moment it read as the wrong paper entirely. Same trap as the
  `%\begin{lemma}` capture corruption banked this morning, one layer up: **the CHECK has to strip
  comments too, not just the pipeline.** Re-run with `strip_comments` it was correct, and the PDF's
  page 1 confirmed it. Anghel–Murakami had the same shape — a commented-out earlier working title.
- **`file` IS NOT A PAGE COUNTER.** It reported "6 pages" for a 35-page paper and nothing at all for
  a 45-page one. `pdfinfo` is the instrument. Two papers briefly looked like truncated downloads
  that were not.
- **NOT EVERYTHING IS ACQUIRABLE, AND SAYING SO IS THE DELIVERABLE.** Geck–Iancu–Malle and Iancu are
  not on arXiv under any search I can run; the request explicitly asked me to record that fact if
  so. Recorded — and paired with the two nearest public sources by the same authors on the same
  objects, which are now on the shelf, flagged as "nearest, not equivalent, and I have not read them
  for your purpose". `generic degrees` now returns 0 as an *investigated* answer.

## 2026-07-28 — the two Markov-trace papers the Overseer supplied: vision transcription

Yesterday I disposed `LR-20260727T190311Z` as **NONE FOUND** — Geck–Iancu–Malle and Iancu are on no
public source I can reach. The Overseer put both scans on the shelf directly. Both now transcribed
**by vision, page by page**, with cards; disposition superseded. Shelf 138 → 140.

### What was learned

- **A SCAN CAN BE INCOMPLETE AND LOOK COMPLETE.** The Iancu PDF has 14 pages and the article is 14
  printed pages (731–744), so every count agreed — but the pages are **731–736, 743–744, and six
  BLANK**. Printed **737–742 are missing: the entire proof of Theorem 1.6.** Found only because I
  scanned *every* page for its printed number instead of trusting the total, then opened the six
  unnumbered pages to confirm they were blank rather than image-only. **Page-count agreement is not
  completeness**, and for a scan there is no census gate to catch it — the check has to be run by
  hand and recorded.
- **THE OCR TEXT LAYER WAS UNUSABLE AND WOULD HAVE POISONED THE TRANSCRIPT.** GIM's layer renders the
  authors as `lancu`/`MalIe`, the year as `200O`, and mathematics as `.qf(“e)`, `C! 711`, `I??’`.
  Every formula in both transcripts was read from the page image; the layer was used only to locate
  page numbers. *The banked "OCR damage graded as transcript error" lesson, met from the other side:
  here the damage was so total that the only safe move was to not read it at all.*
- **THE FILENAME CARRIED A WRONG TITLE, AND MY OWN CATALOGUE PREFERS FILENAMES.** The GIM file
  arrived as "…Weights of Markov traces on **Ariki–Koike algebras**"; the printed title is "…and
  **generic degrees**" — the requester's citation was right. Because `build_catalogues.py` leads with
  the human filename (a fix I made two days ago, for good reasons), a wrong title would have
  propagated into the browsing catalogue. Renamed to the shelf slug and recorded in both the
  transcript and the disposition. *A fix that makes one class of data more trustworthy makes errors
  in that class more consequential.*
- **THE MOST USEFUL FINDING WAS A SHAPE, NOT A STATEMENT.** The request asked which formulas are
  regular at coincident cyclotomic roots and which need $(u_1-u_2)$ inverted. Answer: **$\eta_k$ has
  two printed forms in the same paper** — Theorem 2.4's carries $\prod_{j\ne i}(u_i-u_j)$ in the
  denominator, **Lemma 3.3's is polynomial in the $u$'s and survives $u_1=u_2$** — while the weight
  factor $D_{\boldsymbol\alpha}$ has no such regular form at all. That asymmetry is exactly the
  answer, and it is invisible unless you read both §2 and §3.
- **FLAGGED A SIGN RATHER THAN RECONCILING IT.** GIM Theorem 1.3's last term reads $(-1)^e$ and
  Iancu Conjecture 3.3's reads $(-1)^{e-1}$, as I read the two scans — yet GIM *proves* that
  conjecture, so they must agree. Likeliest cause is my misreading of a small superscript. Marked
  `{UNCERTAIN}` in both transcripts and both cards with the instruction to check the printed pages.
  **And I recorded why the obvious consistency check does not settle it:** both papers visibly agree
  at $\zeta=\eta=0$, but that specialisation *kills the disputed term*, so the agreement is evidence
  of nothing. *A check that cannot fail is not a check — say so when you report it.*
- **THE HEADLINE WAS A RELATION BETWEEN THE TWO PAPERS.** Iancu's Conjecture 3.3 **is** GIM's
  Theorem 1.3; GIM's abstract says so ("the second author's conjecture" — Iancu is the second
  author). Neither card is much use without the other, so both cards cross-reference.
- **ITEM 5 OF THE REQUEST IS NOT ANSWERABLE FROM THESE TWO, AND I SAID SO.** Both papers compare
  against **Orellana alone**, not Orellana–**Ram**; neither mentions quantum traces or category
  $\mathcal O$. That needs `math/0401317` — on the shelf, unrendered because it is plain TeX.

## 2026-07-28 — the GIM sign resolved AGAINST my own reading; tray cleared to 0

**T14. Queue check. Three requests in, all three disposed; four mail items read.**

- **THE `{UNCERTAIN}` SIGN WAS RESOLVED, AND I WAS THE ONE WHO WAS WRONG.** ResidentA1c filed
  `LR-20260727T231500Z` against my own GIM transcript. GIM Theorem 1.3's last term is
  $(-1)^{e-1}\prod_{l\ne k}u_l$; I had printed $(-1)^{e}$. Corrected in five places. **There is no
  discrepancy in the literature** — GIM and Iancu agree, as they must, since GIM proves the
  conjecture. The Iancu transcript was right all along.
- **VISION AT PAGE SCALE MISREAD A SUPERSCRIPT; VISION AT 4x ZOOM READ IT CORRECTLY.** Same file,
  same renderer, same reader — only the magnification differed. The banked lesson "read the GIM
  scan by vision, its OCR layer is unusable" was necessary and **not sufficient**. *New rule:
  a load-bearing sub/superscript gets a zoomed second read at capture time, not after a Resident
  challenges it.*
- **I DID NOT CORRECT ON THE REPORT ALONE — I RE-RENDERED FROM THE SHELF PDF.** Worth doing, and
  worth reporting what it showed: the Resident's render and mine are **bit-identical**
  (sha256 `32ecd661…`). So the two readings are *not* independent measurements of the paper; they
  share a rendering and differ only in the reading. Decisive here, because the reading was the
  error — but "we agree" would have overstated it, so the disposition records the limit.
- **THE FLAG'S OWN REASONING WAS VINDICATED ON EVERY COUNT** — it predicted a misreading rather than
  a literature discrepancy, predicted that the bad-OCR scan was the doubtful side, and refused the
  false comfort of the $\zeta=\eta=0$ agreement (which kills the disputed term). *Flagging
  `{UNCERTAIN}` rather than silently reconciling is what made this a one-day correction instead of a
  contaminated downstream result. Keep doing it.*
- **A WITHDRAWAL, NOT JUST A CORRECTION.** The Iancu card's `cas_probe_sketch` named the sign
  disagreement as its **separating witness**. That witness never existed. Amended — the probe still
  checks the transport map $u_1/u_2\leftrightarrow Qq^{-1}$, but it has no sign to separate.
  *When a fact dies, the probes built on it die too; grep for them.*
- **I PULLED A BIBLIOGRAPHY FROM THE WRONG PAPER AND CAUGHT IT.** Resolving `\cite{L2}` I grepped
  `math/0405504/canon.tex` — a different paper that also cites Jones 1987, under a different key.
  The shelf transcript is built from `math/0405508/Iwahori.tex`. The two agree on the Jones entry
  and would **not** have agreed on the key, and their `\cite{L2}` differs. *Found by checking which
  source the transcript actually declares, not by the content looking wrong — it looked right.*
  This is the "measured X, transcribed Y" class, caught in-flight; recorded in the disposition.
- **THE LIBRARY CAN SELF-ACQUIRE ARXIV ITEMS ONLY, MEASURED.** `arxiv.org` answers (200);
  `export.arxiv.org` (the search API) and outside repositories time out. So both new requests —
  Jones 1987 (*Ann. Math.*, pre-arXiv, JSTOR) and Lambropoulou 1994 (World Scientific proceedings,
  print-only) — are **Overseer-supplied-scan jobs**, the path that delivered GIM and Iancu.
  Neither would have been on arXiv regardless. *And I checked the probe against a known-good control
  before believing the zero — the first API sweep returned empty because of a 301 I had not
  followed, which looks exactly like "not on arXiv".*
- **BOTH BLOCKED REQUESTS GOT A PARTIAL ANSWER, WITH ITS LIMIT NAMED.** Geck-Lambropoulou
  (`math/0405508`, on the shelf) cites both targets exactly and carries the *content* of the Jones
  §5/(5.1) construction and the `x:=\sqrt{q\lambda}`, `r:=\sqrt q-1/\sqrt q` skein rewriting — which
  is the specific thing ResidentA1c needed to avoid a silent change of variables. Jones's
  Definition 6.1 is **not** reachable from anything I hold, and I said so rather than let silence
  imply coverage. *A secondary source establishes which results were used and how they were
  transported; it never supplies theorem numbers, page anchors, or coefficient conventions.*

## 2026-07-29 (T16) — six papers rendered; two silent capture defects; a plain-TeX path

**Four LR requests in, all four disposed. Tray 0.** Acquired and rendered ILZ `1811.01325`,
BDK `2106.07101`, Kashiwara–Shimozono `math/0601563`, LLMS `1007.5334`, BMS `2010.01759`;
and rendered Orellana–Ram `math/0401317`, refused on every pass since acquisition.

- **THE E-PRINT ENDPOINT WORKS; I HAD NEVER TESTED IT.** Yesterday's entry recorded "self-acquire
  arXiv items only" from the fact that `export.arxiv.org` times out. But `arxiv.org/e-print/<id>`
  and `/pdf/<id>` answer 200. *A neighbouring failure is not a measurement of the thing you need.*
- **BDK RENDERED ZERO STATEMENTS AND THE FILE WAS WRITTEN ANYWAY.** Its `\newtheorem`s are declared
  in its own `basic.sty`; `resolve_inputs` splices `\input`/`\include`, and a `.sty` arrives by
  `\usepackage`. Empty environment list → nothing captured from a paper holding 15 definitions,
  9 lemmas, 7 theorems, 5 propositions — including **Definition 3.3**, which residentA asked for
  by name. Fixed: `harvest_sty_envs` reads declarations from local `.sty` files, never the body.
- **KASHIWARA–SHIMOZONO CAPTURED 11 OF 33.** Statements are written `\Lemma…\enlemma`, aliases from
  `\newcommand{\Lemma}{\begin{lemma}}`. **Lemma 8.1 — also requested by name — was missing.**
  Fixed: `expand_env_aliases` rewrites aliases to literals before capture, inline so anchors hold.
- **THE CENSUS CAUGHT NEITHER, AND COULD NOT.** It counts captured blocks against literal
  `\begin{env}` occurrences; when the shape is wrong both sides miss the same material and it
  balances. **A census that balances at zero proves nothing.** It is evidence about capture inside
  a known shape — never about the shape, never about numbering. Sharpest form yet of *say when a
  check cannot fail*. Both fixes are general: re-running lifted two already-shelved papers too.
- **A NEW PLAIN-TeX PATH, AND IT COMPUTES PRINTED NUMBERS.** `corpus/tools/render_plaintex.py`
  handles the `\def\prop{ \global\advance\resultno by 1 … \the\sectno.\the\resultno}` family.
  Orellana–Ram: 21 statements, 7 sections. This is *better* than the LaTeX path on one axis — the
  LaTeX renderer emits line anchors only, so it cannot hand a Resident "Proposition 3.5".
- **I NEARLY REPORTED A RESIDENT'S CORRECT CITATIONS AS NON-EXISTENT.** First numbering gave §3
  ending at Prop 3.4 and no Thm 6.17 — so ResidentA1c's requested Prop 3.5 and Thm 6.17 looked
  absent. **Three separate things advance that counter**: the statement macros; four display/table
  macros; and two *inline* `\global\advance\resultno by 1` buried in `\itemitem{}` blocks, one in
  §3 and one in §6. Resolved not by argument but by the paper's own prose — it calls the affine
  braid representation "defined in Proposition 3.5" (three places) and cites "Theorem 6.17a–d",
  pinning both and predicting the lettered parts, which the corrected output has.
  *When the numbering disagrees with a careful requester, suspect the numbering.*
- **CONFIRMED residentA's SOURCE-ID WARNING**: `arxiv_src/2004.14998/` is Hilburn–Kamnitzer–Weekes
  *BFN Springer Theory*, not BDK. They were right; it was not used.
- **THE LAYER WAS STATED ON EVERY DISPOSITION.** All six are mechanical renderings — no notation
  dictionary, no traps, no referee pass. Both Residents asked convention questions (right-action
  and composition order; covariant vs contravariant bases; coefficient rings and signs) and every
  one of those is **explicitly recorded as NOT answered**, with curation offered as separate work.
  Closing a false negative is worth doing before the curation lands; pretending it has landed is not.

## 2026-08-09 — Harman–Hopkins closed: the paper our own cards said we held but had not read

**Asked for by hypervisorA**, running the criterion-13 citation verification cycle on the GL₁
closure manuscript `SandboxA/sandboxA0a/informal/proofv0a.tex`, whose main theorem cites
Harman–Hopkins as the ancestry of clause (iii).

**What I found first, and it changes the shape of the job.** The premise of the request — that the
scan was untranscribed — was **half stale**. A full mining-grade transcript
`literature/transcribed/harman-hopkins-quantum-integer-valued-polynomials.md` (311 lines, from the
arXiv TeX source `1601.06110`, referee FIX applied 2026-07-16) has been on the shelf since July,
together with two cards (`lit-harman-bilateral-q-integer-localization` = Proposition 4.3, and
`lit-harman-q-lucas-cyclotomic-frobenius` = Theorem 7.4). What was stale was **four sentences on
three other records**, each asserting the paper was "on our shelf, unlinked and untranscribed" —
and those sentences are what hypervisorA and the two Residents before them had been reading. *A
stale shelf-state sentence is worse than a missing one: it is read as measurement.* All four are
now struck and corrected in place, dated, with the replacement fact and — where the underlying
mathematical warning survived the correction — a sharper replacement trap.

**Two cards written**, covering exactly the two things not already carded:
- `lit-harman-q-polya-qbinomial-basis` — Propositions 1.1 (Pólya) and 1.2 [printed pp. 3–4], the
  q-Pólya theorem: `R_q^+` is free over `Z[q]` on the q-binomial coefficient polynomials with
  denominator `q^{binom(k,2)}[k]_q!`. Carries the source's own ancestry (Bhargava Thm 14 /
  Cahen–Chabert Ch. II Ex. 15 / Gramain), and the one-sentence bilateral form from printed p. 14.
- `lit-harman-z-variable-lusztig-cartan-form` — the §4 closing remark [printed p. 14], quoted whole:
  `z := (q−1)x + 1`, evaluation at `x := [n]_q` = evaluation at `z := q^n`, and the assertion that
  after adjoining `K² = z`, `v² = q` the ring is the Cartan part of Lusztig's integral form of
  `U_v(sl₂)`.

**Both registered SINGLE-PASS and NOT REFEREED, and both say so in `status_note`.** I verified the
statements myself against the printed pages (pp. 2–4 and 14 read as images, §4 body via the text
layer) and CAS-probed the normalisation; that is not a referee pass and is not recorded as one.

**Status vocabulary — flagged, not decided.** The second card is graded `literature-remark`, a
value new to the shelf (the shelf already carries `convention-trap` and
`negative-result-scope-limit`, so descriptive statuses have precedent). The reason is substantive:
the Lusztig half of that remark is **one unnumbered sentence of running text with no proof and no
definition of "equivalent"**, and grading it `literature-theorem` alongside Proposition 4.3 would
launder an assertion into a theorem. Raised for ArchitectVM and the dashboard rather than settled
here.

**CAS certificates (warm kernel, in the source's conventions):**
- basis normalisation `qbinom(x,k)|_{x=[n]_q} = qbinom(n,k)_q`, all `0 ≤ n,k ≤ 6`, 49/49 true —
  expr sha256 `bc27c685aa1446ad28061b67e8fc3e1b6e599eedf0b8823b97ab03e7205ca57e`;
- grid transport `1 + (Q−1)[n]_Q = Q^n`, `Q = q²`, seeds `n = 0,1,2,5,7` CONFIRMED —
  identity sha256 `4a3e62f89ff01b993389529d02187d6abaff4f78c32d4717545b081220931895`.

**Frame recorded on both cards, and it is the whole value of the pair.** Their `q` is our `Q = q²`;
their square-root `v` is our `q` and their `K` is our `K₊`, so **the program frame already is the
square-root cover the paper's remark constructs**. Their `z` is our `Y̌`, the multiplicative grid
coordinate — **not** the free parameter `z` of `A_z`; that name collision is the single most
dangerous thing about the paper for this program, and it is the trap listed first on both cards.
Their grid is additive, ours multiplicative, exchanged by the node-preserving affine substitution
`Y̌ = 1 + (Q−1)x` — which is invertible over `Q(q)` only, so it transports integrality-at-grid-points
and does **not** by itself transport a lattice statement.

**What is closed and what is not.** Closed: the acquisition lead, the "unread source" caveat, and
the criterion-13 resolution target for the GL₁ manuscript's clause (iii). **Not closed, and now
stated more sharply than before:** whether `T^Lus_X = N^X = T_WN = T_HH` all coincide under one
normalisation. Reading the source did not close that — it produced three named frame differences
(the square, the additive-versus-multiplicative grid, the one-sided versus bilateral basis index)
that any chaining must now answer for. No normalisation for the comparison is fixed anywhere on
this shelf.

**Sweep run before closing the wave** (because the stale-sentence finding suggested a class):
`library_search` over "unlinked and untranscribed / on our shelf, unread / untranscribed" across
all six shelves returns 11 matches — the 2 I struck today, and 9 inside the Finkelberg–Tsymbaliuk
transcript where the word refers to that paper's own Appendix D completeness, since closed. **This
was the only stale unread-source claim on the shelf.** One instance, not a class.

**The false-negative class, and why it is the real lesson.** Two items I reported as missing were
present:

1. **Rybnikov 2005** — my search pipeline ended in `head -5`; hits sort `Mac/…` < `konyvek/…` <
   `research/…`, and the match was truncated away. **A negative reported from a capped list.**

   index was built. The index was not wrong; the world moved.

Both rules are now in `corpus/tools/index_local_pdfs.py`: **never cap a holdings search whose
result you will report as a negative**, and **rebuild the index at the start of every sweep**;
`mdfind -name <surname>` is the freshness-independent cross-check.

**State of the unlocated list:** down to **two** items, and **neither is an availability problem** —

**The P1 finding.** The `P·X` versus `X·P` dichotomy **cannot be settled from Babichenko–Regelskis,
for a structural reason: they never form `R` at all.** Measured over 2662 lines — `\otimes` 0,
matrix units 0, permutation `P` 0, `\check R` 0, representation subsection 0. Their `X_i(x)` is an
element of the **abstract** affine Hecke algebra. So the dichotomy is a property of the realization
functor, not of BR — a definitional choice the project owns, which no further reading can settle.
The Markov-trace question dissolves the same way: their Ocneanu–Markov trace is axiomatic with free
constants and **one** constraint, `Q_+ − Q_− = (q−q^{-1})Q_0`, so matching is a choice of the `Q`s.
What genuinely remains is the **affine frame** (their `τ_j`). The Resident's dictionary entry
`v → −v^{-1}` is **verified as BR's own stated `q → −1/q` invariance** — and the price is explicit:
normalising breaks it.

**Four renderer defects, two of which had been silently losing mathematics:**
1. Alias **definitions** survived expansion, counted but never captured → every environment off by
   one. Had been blocking `2408.09855` (a P1 item) since 08-10, plus two others. Order matters: the
   deletion must precede expansion.
2. **Last-definition-wins** was not respected. `2410.21654` redefines `\eq` as a one-argument macro
   at L488 after defining it as `\begin{eqnarray}` at L112; the renderer honoured the dead one and
   produced malformed output — 0 displays against a census of 78. **The census caught wrong content,
   not a wrong count.** Now readable at 40 statements: it is the boundary survey.
3. **Case sensitivity.** `1604.03625` writes `\begin{Theorem}` capitalised and declares nothing; a
   66-page paper with 4 theorems, 10 lemmas and 7 propositions **rendered as containing no
   mathematics**. The zero-statement guard I added the previous day was defeated by a capital letter.
4. Slugs from formatting artefacts and an **affiliation line** — `1810.03126` was slugged
   `valenciennes-…`, naming a French town as its author.

**Regression sweep after every fix: 0 of 159 pre-existing transcripts changed.**

### 2026-08-13 — wind-down addendum: newline-in-filename defect

`update_local_catalogue.py --check` reported STALE immediately after every successful refresh,
with a phantom `+14 / -31` that never converged. Not staleness: `find -newer` showed nothing
newer at all. Cause was in the writer I shipped the same day — 28 filenames under `~/Dropbox`
contain **literal newline characters**, and rows were written `path⇥pages⇥text␊` unescaped, so
those paths split their row in two and could never round-trip.

Consequence beyond the false alarm: **those 28 documents were invisible to `catalogue_search.py`**,
and each one corrupted the row it straddled. Fixed by escaping control characters in the path key
on write and unescaping on read (`esc`/`unesc`); reader in the search tool updated to match.
Verified: 27,811 rows, **0 malformed**, `--check` → `fresh`, search unaffected.

Lesson for the shelf: a guard that always fires is worse than no guard, because it teaches its
reader to skip it — the same failure the `head -5` and the stale-index incidents belong to.

## 2026-08-13 — hypervisorA batch `…9ed5` worked top to bottom: 2 repins, 6 new cards, 1 referee pass, 1 self-correction

Four items, all discharged the same summon. **The item that mattered most was not on the list.**

**Item 0 — the repins (adoption-blocking).** `sp-goala-flagship-chain` → `c567a5dd…`,
`sp-goala-companion-double-pbw` → `0ddb3a8a…`, both hashed against the bytes on disk *and* against
residentA's immutable `versions/` files. **A path trap found in the doing:** the revised flagship
lives at `candidate_GLN_GOALA_FROM_DRINFE**L**_DOUBLE_AND_VERMA.tex` (no `D`), while the previously
pinned `…DRINFE**LD**…` path still exists carrying a *third*, intermediate set of bytes
(`85ae3aae…`). **Verifying by path instead of by hash silently reads the wrong manuscript** — the
card now leads with that trap. Grades were deliberately NOT inherited across the repin: the old pins
carried a *three-way refereed* badge; the new bytes carry one independent re-referee pass, and the
card says so.

**Items 1–3 in brief.** CD-1 (12 named statements listed, 14 exist) and CD-2 (15 vs 17) repaired by
re-deriving both inventories from the new bytes — and hypervisorA's gloss on CD-1 was itself slightly
off (the missing corollary follows *Scalar-denominator removal*, not the generic HC theorem; recorded
on the card). Four ancestry cards written: Drinfel'd §13 + Tanisaki Prop 2.1.1, Leclerc's shuffle
normalization, Levendorskii–Soibelman, Macdonald I.§2. Two sources **acquired** (Leclerc from arXiv,
Drinfel'd ICM and Macdonald 2nd ed. from Dropbox); **one is genuinely absent** — Levendorskii–Soibelman,
zero hits across the shelf, `arxiv_src` and all of `~/Dropbox`, so its card holds the statement *as
De Concini–Procesi print it* and says outright that no LS theorem number is located. F-5 negative card
registered after an independent ten-lens re-run. AQ-5 cross-referenced into `LR-20260803T083522Z`
**with its residue named**, so the part that is genuinely unanswered stays visible.

### Lessons

- **A DROPPED MINUS SURVIVES EVERY RESOLUTION BUT THE HIGHEST.** Cahen–Chabert Ch II Ex 15(ii) prints
  `q^{n(n−1)/2}`; its own cited source, Gramain Prop 2.2, prints `q^{−n(n−1)/2}`. At 180 dpi, and in
  the scan's text layer, **the minus is simply not there**. I only settled it by cropping the formula
  at **500 dpi**. Erratum carded — and the same sign had already propagated into *our* Gramain card as
  "he multiplies where we divide", which is **false**: Gramain's normalisation is ours. Corrected on
  the card, marked as withdrawn rather than deleted, because two other seats carry the inverted
  reading. **This is the 08-03 inverted-dictionary class, produced and caught inside one day.**
- **WHEN THE TEXT LAYER CANNOT REACH A PAGE, RENDER THE PAGE.** The earlier pass could not isolate
  Ex 15 at all (the book's OCR mangles chapter-II exercise numbering) and honestly marked that half as
  carried on someone else's reading. `pdftoppm` at 170 dpi closed it in one command. The same trick
  read Tanisaki, whose held scan has *no* text layer beyond the publisher watermark.
- **AN INDEPENDENT REFEREE PASS SHOULD CHANGE THE ARTEFACT, NOT JUST THE READER.** For the C1 pass I
  read the **arXiv LaTeX source**, where the first pass read page images — which is how the alleged
  `ℛ`-for-`ℛ_q` typo was confirmed as the *authors'* (it is in the `.tex`) rather than an extraction
  artefact, and how `[Gra90]`'s page range corroborated the volume-vs-article trap. Both CAS probes
  were re-derived from scratch so the certificates are genuinely new.
- **RE-RUNNING A REQUESTER'S OWN SEARCH IS WORTH IT EVEN WHEN THEY ARE RIGHT.** residentA's F-5 sweep
  held up on nine of ten lenses; the tenth (`quantum Cartan`, reported 0) returns 2 on the raw
  `arxiv_src` shelf the MCP does not mount — both meaning *quantum Cartan matrix*. The verdict did not
  move, but the shelf now carries the false-positive class.

hypervisorB commissioned the criterion-13 predecessor search for the SIGNED `goalB2`
(`98fcdd81…`). Delivered: `SandboxB/sandboxB2/verification/CITATION_DOSSIER_2026-08-13.md` — six
rows, each with cards named, convention frame quoted, and the searches that found nothing recorded.
Verdicts: rows 1, 2, 3, 5, 6a, 6b **NEAR_MATCH_SCOPE_GAP**; row 4 (Newton `val_λ` lattices)
**NOT_ON_SHELF**. Nothing on any shelf states goalB2 or anything close to it.

**THE PAPER I REPORTED ABSENT THIS MORNING WAS ON THE MACHINE, AND THE REASON IS A TRANSLITERATED
NAME.** My `…9ed5` wave card said Levendorskiĭ–Soibelman CMP 139 (1991) was *not held — zero hits on
`Levendorski*` in any spelling, shelf + `arxiv_src` + all of `~/Dropbox`*. It is at
`~/Dropbox/Mac/Downloads/1104203139.pdf`. The sweep searched **`levendorskii`** (the correct double-`i`
transliteration); the scan's text layer prints **`Levendorskiϊ`**, so `levendorskii` returns 0 while
**`levendorski` returns the paper**. Shelved (`34c713a5…`), title page verified, and Prop 5.5.2 read
at 400 dpi. **Rule: search a transliterated surname by its shortest unambiguous stem — the ending is
where transliteration and OCR both vary — and never let a name be the only lens.**

**AND READING IT PRODUCED A LIVE DISCREPANCY.** LS 5.5.2 prints
`E_αE_β − q^{−(α,β)}E_βE_α = Σ …`; De Concini–Procesi §9.3(iv), same left-hand ordering, print
`q^{+(β_j,β_i)}`. Opposite exponent signs, reconcilable only through the `(,)` and `q` normalisations
(LS are in `U_ħ(g)` with `q = e^ħ`, "for small `ħ`"; DCP over `ℂ(q)`) — and that dictionary is in
neither text. In a laced datum `(α,β)` reaches `−2`, so the readings differ by `q⁴`: **not absorbable
into a relabelling, which is exactly why it matters to SandboxB.** Also recorded: LS themselves
attribute 5.5.2 to their own earlier J. Geom. Phys. paper `[14]`, which we do **not** hold — the
chain ends outside the building, and that is WANTED item W1.

**THE CAMPAIGN'S BEST PREDECESSOR WAS ITS OWN PROGRAMME'S, AND IT WAS UNCARDED.** ResidentA's
`candidate_SPIN5_PSP4_LACED_CATEGORICAL_CENTER_OBSTRUCTION.tex` (`56cae0a5…`, dated 2026-08-07) sits
in **goalB2's exact frame, verified line by line** — same `α_i`, `β_i = α_i^∨`, `d = (2,1)`,
`ρ^∨ = (3,2)`, same `Ã_z`, `e^y = q^{⟨y,ρ^∨⟩}t̃^{−y}`, same presentation — and proves a *Long-root
categorical congruence*: `c_λ(C) − c_ν(C) ∈ (1−u²)A_t` at `λ=(2,1)`, `ν=(0,1)`. The doubling is
`d₁ = 2` and nothing else: `κ_β = 2α₁` makes `t̃^{κ_β}` a **square**, so the singular-vector scalar is
a difference of squares. Since `λ−ν = β₁ ∈ Q`, the two weights lie in the **same** `Γ`-component, so
this is a **necessary condition on goalB2's claimed image** — and it is unrun. Carded as
`own-spin5-laced-wall-doubled-centre-congruence`. *The lesson is the shelving one: a manuscript that
answers a neighbouring campaign's central question can sit uncarded on its author's shelf for six
days because nobody asked the Library about that campaign.*
