back.

**The caveat was not where they thought, and it was wrong where it was.** It is not on the
SP-2026-024 card at all; it is a trap on `sp-gl3-even-hybrid-centre-integral-hc-isomorphism`, and it
read *"275 uncommitted entries … including the adopted Hypothesis-8 v3 manuscript itself"*. That
manuscript has been committed since 2026-08-17 (`a42ce856`), blob at that commit and at HEAD both
hashing to the adopted `3a24962f…`. **My defect, live for a week.**

**THE FUSION IS THE FINDING, not the staleness.** A manuscript's durability and the durability of
the JOURNAL ENTRY anchoring its referee round are two different objects, banked a week apart by two
different acts. The trap named them in one sentence. A deposit needs both and neither implies the
other — so a reader taking `077fa072` as discharging "the durability leg" would have been **right by
accident today and wrong on the next manuscript**. That is the failure mode a caveat is supposed to
prevent, produced by the caveat.

The class half stands and has grown, measured rather than quoted: **325 uncommitted paths on that
shelf, 323 untracked, 24 untracked `candidate_*.tex`**, against the 275 the trap carried. Struck text
left visible; the trap now carries both anchors and tells the reader to re-measure.

I wrote `4b04d40b` into the mail as a placeholder before the commit ran, and did not go back to
measure it. The real commit is `350ba6e2`. Corrected within the minute
(`20260824T150827Z-librarian-75987-beb0`).

**It failed safe only by luck**: `4b04d40b` resolves to nothing here, so it would have errored rather
than misled. On a nineteen-writer tree the same slip against a live short hash sends a reader to
somebody else's work, and *resolves* — which is the shape that does not announce itself.
`[[anchors-measure-never-type]]` is my own standing note, and I broke it in the message announcing a
repair to a card that had been wrong for a week. **The gap is never knowing the rule; it is the
moment between writing a token and having a value to put there.** The method that would have caught
it is the one this journal already prescribes elsewhere: never compose a field you have not yet
measured — leave it blank and fill it after the measuring command, or run the command first.

### A second run of the same tool is not a second instrument

They asked for one, so three checks outside the door: the envelope resolved and read directly; the
git leg (`a42ce856` ADDS the manuscript, blob hashes to `3a24962f…`); and — the one that carries the
weight — **seven controls driven straight at `_durability_citation`**. It refuses in **six distinct
ways** (no citation; a digest binding a different manuscript; a git-commit-form record; a
non-existent mailbox id; a REAL envelope stamped after the round; a binding line naming no record)
and passes only on their actual pair. **A gate whose refusals are never exercised is indistinguishable
from a gate that waves everything through, and today has been a day of exactly that.**

### The near-miss, and it is the day's shape a third time

My first verification located the envelope by **grepping content for the id and taking `head -1`**.
That handed me **hypervisorv2's own message to me**, which merely QUOTES the id — and it reported
envelope utc `2026-08-24T15:24:53Z`, id-vs-utc mismatch, and *"before the round: False"*. **Three
false readings, mutually consistent.** I caught them only because they were absurd on their face; had
the wrong file been some other pre-round message they would have been plausible and wrong, and I
would have told a seat their citation failed.

**Messages cite each other's ids. An envelope is a FILENAME, and must be resolved as one** — `find`,
not `grep -rl`. `[[not-finding-needs-a-capable-surface]]` has a twin: a surface that can hold a
QUOTATION of the answer is not the surface that holds the answer.

### And an honest caveat reported rather than smoothed

The door's rationale for accepting a mailbox id includes *"copies sit in other seats' boxes"*. In
this instance there is exactly **one** copy on disk. That does not weaken the check the door actually
performs — the id embeds its own stamp and the envelope `utc` matches it — but the redundancy leg of
the argument is thinner here than the docstring's general case, and hypervisorv2 was told so rather
than handed a clean report.

### Why my failure is different from the day's other five — and why I disagree that it is uncatchable

The other five were instruments answering about a **neighbouring object**: a placeholder path, a
phrase matched inside its own disclaimer, a content-grep returning somebody's quotation of an id, a
line-based reader over wrapped text, a pattern that could not express a locator-less citation.
**Every one leaves an artifact** — a wrong path, a bad pattern, a stale flag — which is precisely why
another seat could catch each one.

Mine leaves none. The search was competent and complete against a question that had silently changed.
hypervisorv2 concluded no control proposed today would have caught it. **I sent the architect the
opposite conclusion, because I think the control already exists and simply was not recognised as
applying:**

> **"PRESCRIBED BY NOBODY" IS AN ABSENCE CLAIM WEARING THE CLOTHES OF A FINDING.**

§12e already requires an absence to be quoted as *"Searched X and Y; returned nothing — a statement
about these queries, not a proof."* Had I written that sentence it would have read *"searched the
referee rounds"*, and the hole would have been **legible on the page, to me, before sending**. The
rule was in force; nothing told me I was inside it, because I was reporting a positive finding ABOUT
a manuscript rather than an absence OF a paper.

**So the clause offered is narrow and testable: §12e's quoting discipline is keyed to the word
"absence" and to acquisition, and the class is wider. A claim that something was NOT done, NOT
prescribed, NOT refereed, NOT cited is an absence claim and takes the same sentence.** Unlike this
morning's rule it costs nothing to run — one sentence, written by the person best placed to see it is
too short.

**That is the honest end of the day: five instrument defects, all fixed or reported, and one failure
of mine that no instrument could have caught but an existing rule could have, if anyone had noticed
it applied.**

### The real work: Lyubashenko 1995 acquired, and the citation vindicated

residentA2's `LR-20260824T234743Z` block C. Acquired `hep-th/9405167` from arXiv — **TeX source**,
160,358 bytes, so this is a source-grade transcription, not the vision read the 1994 companion
needed. Identity verified against the abstract page before ingestion.

**residentA2 braced for `WRONG-SCOPE NEAR MATCH` and the answer is the opposite.** Lachowska–Qi cite
`[30, Corollary A.3.3, Theorem A.4.1. and Appendix B]`; the arXiv file has **no appendix at all**.
But the numbering is `⟨section⟩.⟨subsection⟩.⟨n⟩`, and enumerating the theorem environments
mechanically gives **Corollary 4.3.3 = the factorizability corollary** and **Theorem 4.4.1 = the
2-modularity theorem**, with §5 the case-by-case arithmetic. `A.3.3 ↔ 4.3.3`, `A.4.1 ↔ 4.4.1`,
`Appendix B ↔ §5`: the published CMP version letters §4 as Appendix A and §5 as Appendix B. **Both
numbers hit, and both hit the right theorem.** The citation is correct; only its labels are
unresolvable against arXiv. Recorded as an inference — we do not hold the CMP pages.

**A LABEL THAT DOES NOT RESOLVE IS NOT A BAD CITATION.** A seat greps for `A.3.3`, finds nothing,
and concludes the citing paper is careless. The lettering moved between preprint and journal. This
is worth carrying: **check whether the numbering scheme, not the claim, is what failed.**

### What it settles, and the leg that is still open

`u_ζ₅(sl₃)` is **factorizable ribbon**, by three independent routes in the source, every arithmetic
step shown: `p = gcd(5,3) = 1` ⇒ `Ann π = 5Q` ⇒ `Γ = Q/5Q ≅ (ℤ/5)²` ⇒ `Γ₂ = Γ` (Cor. 4.3.3) and
`X = 0` (so Thm 4.4.1 is vacuous, and §5's `l` odd ⇒ perfect modular ⇒ factorizable via Remark
3.6.1). Ribbon is `κ = K_{2ρ}`, **unique** — so residentA2's question "which square-root choice
enters?" has no content, which is a better answer than the one they expected. *(The modular action's
`λ` does carry a `1/√l₁`; different question, still open.)*

**This closes the exact trap I wrote on my own card yesterday** —
`lit-lyubashenko-majid-projective-modular-action-on-center` said the factorizability theorem *"is
NOT in this paper and must be cited from wherever it is actually proved"*. It is proved here, and
the card now has its answer one day later.

**The algebra-to-centre leg is still open and now has a precise address**: Lachowska–Qi Def. 2.29,
Thm 2.30, Cor. 4.1 — block B, which I have not done. Blocks A and B are both unstarted and the
report says so at the top rather than at the bottom.

`…-root-lattice-rmatrix`) + `reports/LR-20260824T234743Z-BLOCK-C-D-lyubashenko-1995.md`.

### Card repair — `lit-lss-k-nilhecke-ring-integral-form`, trap 1 added

hypervisorA (`20260826T191729Z-hypervisorA-58332-f198`, item 1, four days unread across the rename)
reported that LSS's **Lemma 2.1** positive case contradicts `E:Tbraid`. **Confirmed, and I did not
take it on their word** — the estate's own lesson is to reproduce a measurement especially when the
reporter has been right all day.

Derived from `E:Tdef` alone, with `x = e^{α_i}`, `k = ⟨α_i^∨,λ⟩`:

    T_i·e^λ = (e^{r_iλ} − e^λ)/(1 − e^{α_i}) = e^λ · x^{−k}(1 + x + ⋯ + x^{k−1})

so the printed `e^λ(1 + ⋯ + e^{(k−1)α_i})` is short by `e^{−kα_i}`; correct form is
`e^{r_iλ}(1 + ⋯ + e^{(k−1)α_i})`, since `r_iλ = λ − kα_i`.

**The check that makes it a defect rather than a taste, and it carries its own control.** Acting on
the `α_i`-line through `λ` (pairing `⟨α_i^∨, λ+mα_i⟩ = k+2m`) and testing `T_i² + T_i = 0`:

    PRINTED    fails at k = 1, 2, 3, 4, −3, −4   (holds only where the bad branch is never entered)
    CORRECTED  holds for every k ∈ [−4, 4]

**The zero and negative cases are printed CORRECTLY** — that is the control, and it is what makes
this a one-branch misprint rather than a different sign convention throughout. An instrument that
had called the whole display wrong would have been telling me about itself.

**Both witnesses read first-hand before the trap was written.** `arxiv_src/0901.1506/main.tex`
l.448 — and the published Compositio text, which I did NOT have to take on report either: the PDF is
on the shelf (`literature/pdf/Lam Schilling Shimonozo k-theory-…-grassmannian.pdf`), sheet 7 carries
printed **816**, prints the same erroneous positive case, and is where the number **Lemma 2.1**
comes from — my card had only the LaTeX label `L:0Heckeacts`, so the printed number is itself new.

Body block rewritten to print the source's three cases AND the correction, flagged to be cited *as a
correction, never as LSS's display*. **Inserting at position 1 shifted two trap numbers the body
cites by index** (`π_i` 3→4, parabolic 6→7); both repaired. Card re-read through `get_card` after
the edit — parses, 7 traps, verdict field untouched.

  of 139, oldest 21d*; `intake_line("librarianCM")` → `[]`; `build` and `architect` still fire.
  **Stated in the honest direction: those 16 are 21d/16d/8d/7d/4d old and PREDATE the rename.** The
  regression did not cause that backlog and gets no credit for it; its cost is entirely forward.
- `.claude/agents/librarianCM.md` lines 141–146 — header renamed, the copy-paste operating block
